     "CI-PRIORITY-NO-VERDICT-CANCEL (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch215.md#P654 at b7b37bfda0, part 3), line numbers at 61afd4f2db, amending carry 4 (CI-QUEUE-PRIORITY-BUILT-IN) with the local shim's measured result (s14 ci-priority FINDINGS, 2026-11-11 20:15-20:42Z). 21 cancels of queued non-priority runs drained no priority run: priority_queued held at 19-20, and GitHub completed about 2 runs per hour. -    "Moved from R10-COP-GATE-E carry 1 (0-based) by the carries-open audit (orchestrator s13, 2026-11-09): not measurable from the recorded fixtures (PR 3371); a round-11 D15 input that waits on named owner data. Write it into the round-11 list. Text verbatim:\n\nRe-routed from R10-COP-GATE-D carry 8 by the s12 carry-routing seat (2026-11-07). Original text: Unanswered residual of R10-CEILING-PROBE Carry 1, from #2465 (handoff-body/r10-ceiling-probe 61b216fc; orchestrator s9 2026-10-31). The gain between set-point and drawn power at levels between the duty gate and the ceiling is unmeasured: the probe measures only the draw at the ceiling, as its approved note scopes it. #2465 routed this gap to R10-DEMAND-OVERBOOK, which is done, and carry_audit refuses a done destination. It is re-routed here because R10-COP-GATE is the live group that owns the DrawRange and power modelling and runs after the probe. Measure first: from the probe's stored samples plus ordinary operation, report whether drawn power tracks the set-point linearly between gate and ceiling, with a flat-price null control. Only if it does not, build the correction, or record a refusal with the number. The measurement needs no new owner data if the recorded fixtures carry enough operating points: drive it with a harness on the recorded fixtures (`dev/audit/harnesses/draw_range_evidence.py` and `dev/audit/harnesses/r10_cop_gate_top.py` are the starting instruments) and report the fitted gain between set-point and drawn power over the band between the duty gate and the ceiling, with the flat-price null. If the fixtures cannot span that band, the body states exactly which readings are missing (set-point, drawn power, band, count of steps) and the owner data that closes it (a diagnostics download with the week of debug data on, from an install whose pump ran across the band), and the carry stays open on that named data, not on a deferral. Carry: R10-COP-GATE-E",
-    "From triage batch 141 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch141.md#P545 at aac4c4a441), the round's playbook destination for lessons that need no instrument. Four lessons for the final playbook: (1) Stack a later part only under the stack study's named conditions, at the parent's merge verdict, never at its handoff; a replay of round 10's chains moved completion by 0.00 to 0.06 h in every arm. (2) A cloud seat's fire is logged with the run's session id read back from list_runs about 2 min after the fire, never with the one-shot trigger id alone, which later answers 404 and cannot tell a fire that ran from one that never did. (3) An owner order with a trigger condition goes into the orchestrator's pending list the turn it is given, with its condition, and a session rotation carries that list; a stamp ordered on a merge waited 13 h, and a workflow re-enable recorded only as prose waited 9.5 h. (4) roster_edit.py append-carry takes no --reason; read back the drain's refusal for every queued op, since a refused line is never retried.",
-    "From r10-fixer-md-spelling-corpus, body items 1d630dea and a629d56d: the round playbook's reflection of the fixer.md sentence #3397 landed in step 2 (merged 04dfcafccdde1ee5df21a02fe4f06a5bc1083ea0) is owed: \"A guard that classifies text by shape is driven over a generated spelling corpus (case, quoting, glued options, delimiters, wrapping) with the corpus size named in the body; a spelling the reviewer finds is added to it.\" R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK-FINAL writes the playbook, so it carries the same rule in the playbook's fixer section, word for word or by citing fixer.md step 2. Moved from R10-FIXER-MD-SPELLING-CORPUS carries 2 and 6 by orchestrator ruling 2026-11-11.",
-    "PLAYBOOK-FREEZE-CLAUSE (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch218.md#P664 at bb3a0e5b92). Each round-11 machinery group states which of the three grounds it claims. The definition is policy wording, and it binds only on tvofi's approving review at merge. Cite only what brief_lint.mjs resolves. The study's scorecard is not in the tree, so it is not cited unless a tracked copy lands first.",
-    "CLOSURE-SOAK-BEFORE-V10 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch218.md#P667 at bb3a0e5b92), owner ruling (tvofi 2026-11-11, verbatim \"I agree with all the recommendations.\", study review Q2). When the last product pull request of round 10 merges, an ordinary patch release is cut. tvofi runs it on their own install while documentation and closeout finish. v10.0.0 is stamped only after tvofi has opened the card's tabs on that install and said so. -    "R11-INTAKE-SCORECARD-PARAGRAPHS (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch218.md#P668 at bb3a0e5b92), owner ruling (tvofi 2026-11-11, verbatim \"I agree with all the recommendations.\", study review Q3; this ruling is the policy approval). -    "R11-STANDING-SEAT-RETIREMENT (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch235.md#P709 at be86e36a56), owner ruling (tvofi 2026-11-12, verbatim \"Plan them for round 11\", on batch 234 P708). -    "From r10-outside-learner-robustness-p2, body item 4b518e5d: Round 11: the scaled settling tail. Measured on `dev/audit/harnesses/boost_press_tail_walk.py` (table above, both slopes, with the heat-free `freeze` twins as the null control): neither formula keeps every press arm within `press_120x1_full`'s |worst| on `tests/boost_drift_replay.py`'s replay. The freeze with no heat walks the scale as far as the press does (-0.0232 vs -0.0237), so the walk is the freeze's sample selection, not the heat. The walk is also not monotone in the tail (press 10x1: +0.0028 at a 1.5 h tail, -0.0237 at 4.55 h). Precondition a future formula must meet: every press arm of the harness within the absolute bound under `slope<new>` at its own merge base, re-measured and not carried from these figures, plus an explanation of the freeze-skip walk (why a heat-free freeze of these hours moves the interval learner, and why the formula's arms are not tuned to this scenario's hours). The data such a formula prices on already ships: `BoostState.space_boosted`, persisted as `space_settle/boosted_h`. Carry: R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK-FINAL (items 4b518e5d)",
-    "Round-11 opening list, moved from R10-PREPR-RESIDUE by the orchestrator ruling of 2026-11-12 (self-fed, would move closure): (1) extend prepr.sh body-check so a return is refused for the classes open_pr still refuses; (2) one grammar owner for Forward-carry between policy_lint.mjs and carry_audit.py; (3) ci_predict lacks the instrument-self-tests prediction behind an instrument-self-tests red; (4) a predicting detector for the HEAD-ONLY class, since step 7c reads pushed reds only; (5) ci_predict labels an import of an INERT file as INERT READS and prescribes apply-inert; (6) ci_predict carry arm grades roster-branch-only destinations bad when the roster ref is not fetched; (7) split the never tips of open_prepr_receipts by the Prepr-absent reason.",
-    "Round-11 opening list (or round 10 if the owner approves the macOS runner spend and the replan shows it does not move closure): un-defer R10-PREPR-DARWIN-SELFTEST-LANE with this widened brief. +    "`policy_budgets.json`: stale: the README's caps are keyed `tools/audit/README.md`, a path the tree no longer has, and are matched through the move map. A reader grepping the new path finds no cap. Carry: R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK-FINAL [moved: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE]",
+    "Moved from R10-COP-GATE-E carry 1 (0-based) by the carries-open audit (orchestrator s13, 2026-11-09): not measurable from the recorded fixtures (PR 3371); a round-11 D15 input that waits on named owner data. Write it into the round-11 list. Text verbatim:\n\nRe-routed from R10-COP-GATE-D carry 8 by the s12 carry-routing seat (2026-11-07). Original text: Unanswered residual of R10-CEILING-PROBE Carry 1, from #2465 (handoff-body/r10-ceiling-probe 61b216fc; orchestrator s9 2026-10-31). The gain between set-point and drawn power at levels between the duty gate and the ceiling is unmeasured: the probe measures only the draw at the ceiling, as its approved note scopes it. #2465 routed this gap to R10-DEMAND-OVERBOOK, which is done, and carry_audit refuses a done destination. It is re-routed here because R10-COP-GATE is the live group that owns the DrawRange and power modelling and runs after the probe. Measure first: from the probe's stored samples plus ordinary operation, report whether drawn power tracks the set-point linearly between gate and ceiling, with a flat-price null control. Only if it does not, build the correction, or record a refusal with the number. The measurement needs no new owner data if the recorded fixtures carry enough operating points: drive it with a harness on the recorded fixtures (`dev/audit/harnesses/draw_range_evidence.py` and `dev/audit/harnesses/r10_cop_gate_top.py` are the starting instruments) and report the fitted gain between set-point and drawn power over the band between the duty gate and the ceiling, with the flat-price null. If the fixtures cannot span that band, the body states exactly which readings are missing (set-point, drawn power, band, count of steps) and the owner data that closes it (a diagnostics download with the week of debug data on, from an install whose pump ran across the band), and the carry stays open on that named data, not on a deferral. Carry: R10-COP-GATE-E [moved: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE]",

     "PART 2 ITEM unless the R10-PREPR-RESIDUE fixer dispatched 2026-11-11 00:41Z has not yet handed off when this carry lands: that fixer's brief was rendered before this carry, so a fixer already past handoff leaves it to the group's next part.\nFrom triage batch 148 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch148.md#P557 at a515e249d5), queue line S14-BODY-PUSH-LANDED-MAIN-ONLY, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. tools/audit/seat/body_push.sh lines 182-184 (CARRY 11, P287) state that a body carry its group does not yet hold 'on main's roster or the round's branch' is refused, but line 187 runs carry_audit.py --roster-ref origin/main landed, and bus.sh does the same at lines 236, 241 and 256; cloud_dispatch renders briefs from the round branch, so a carry added since the last roster landing on main is refused CARRY-LANDED (exit 4) and the relay's carry-fold folds 0 items and re-fails every pass. Fix: tools/audit/carry_audit.py landed counts a carry landed when its group holds it on main's roster OR on the round branch (the ref roster_lib.default_roster_ref names when the clone has it); body_push.sh and bus.sh drop the hard-coded --roster-ref origin/main so one rule serves both.      "PART 2 ITEM unless the R10-PREPR-RESIDUE fixer dispatched 2026-11-11 00:41Z has not yet handed off when this carry lands: that fixer's brief was rendered before this carry, so a fixer already past handoff leaves it to the group's next part.\nFrom triage batch 152 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch152.md#P563 at 8d9dc31d9b), queue line S14-LANDED-PR-NEEDS-ROSTER-PR, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. bus.sh carry_landed (line 229 at 63bcdcaad8) runs carry_audit.py landed and land-ops with only --pr \"#$1\" (line 237), and carry_audit own_group (line 587) skips every '#'-prefixed source (line 593), so a dispatch never resolves the PR's own group: a fresh PR whose body carries a status line on its own group's carries is refused CARRY-MISSING at its first reviewer dispatch. Measured on PR 3424's body (head fix/r10-train-residue 175556657a) at origin/handoff/audit-r10-roster: --pr '#3424' rc 1, 3 missing; --pr '#3424' --topic fix/r10-train-residue rc 0, 0 missing (3 CARRY-STATUS). carry_audit never reads resume.pr, so running roster_sync after open would change nothing. Fix: carry_landed reads .head.ref in its existing gh api pulls/$1 call and passes it as --topic to landed and land-ops, so own_group resolves by pr_group_of (group_for_branch, then without a trailing -pr), as audit --branch does (line 980). Lands beside P557 (s6-batch148.md#P557) in the same carry_landed call, which replaces its hard-coded --roster-ref origin/main. Self-test: the 3424 shape (--pr plus head ref, own-group status line) passes at the round ref; null control: a finding routed to another group that does not hold it is still CARRY-MISSING; a mutant dropping --topic fails the first case.      "GATE-LOCK-PRIORITY-ENV-LEAK (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch220.md#P673 at 924a4de8ed), line numbers at 61afd4f2db. tools/pr/prepr.sh :4159-4161 exports HPO_GATE_PRIORITY, and in tests/gate_lock.py `_priority_cases` the `normal` waiter of the two ordering arms (:1628, :1738) enters `take` with no `_Env(None)`, so it inherits the caller's priority. It moves the dispatch moment: a topic-keyed record re-keyed by `open_pr.sh`, a relay that posts a held verdict, and policy text in `standing-dispatch.md` and `fix-review.md` step 7. `fix-review.md` is edited by R10-ORCH-REVIEW-PACKET (after this group, at zero headroom), and the packet is rendered at the same dispatch moment, so the moment and its policy text land together there. This PR's pieces it builds on: `dispatch --nonce` re-records a minted nonce, and REVIEW OWED already applies `app_approve.sh --carry`. Controls to carry: a handoff-dispatched verdict whose PR head is the handoff tip plus a clean main merge carries and posts (null: a PR head with a conflict resolution refuses the carry and the PR is re-dispatched at its head); a verdict for a topic that never opens a PR is reported, never posted; one mutant that posts without the carry check fails the conflict arm.",

The estimated catch rate of a class-enumeration line is one third of class blocks, an estimate, since class enumeration stays judgement.\nFix: tools/audit/seat/cloud_dispatch.py (add it to this group's files) renders two generated lines into every fixer brief: the live consumer groups, being the reverse after-edges and the groups sharing files, read from roster_lib, as the only valid Carry: targets; and the finding's class-enumeration command. tools/pr/prepr.sh prints both so the fixer sees them before the handoff.\nFailing test first: a self-test arm rendering the brief for a fixture group with one live consumer and one done consumer; red before the lines exist, green with the live consumer listed and the done one absent.\nMutation proof: dropping the done-group filter, or dropping the class-enumeration line, turns the arm red.\nNull control: a group with no consumers and no enumeration command renders the brief byte-identical to today.\nCarry: R10-DISPATCH-AT-HANDOFF",

Only the mechanised parts are this carry; the recycle caps (standing seats at 150k context, fixer and repair seats at 200k (L and XL fixers: about 300k, per the 2026-10-24 caps), through a STATE file) are orchestrator practice with no roster or tooling op. Note: R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK carry 0 records the owner's 2026-10-24 caps by role (about 300k for L and XL fixers); the 200k figure is tvofi's 2026-11-01 amendment and must be reconciled there, not here.\nMeasured: repair seats are 190.8M of the 598.6M weighted s9 local tokens (32 percent); an opus repair seat averages 11.5M against 2.3M for a sonnet one; R10-FLY-RAISE-REVIEWABLE has fixerModel sonnet yet its round-2 fixer ran opus at 20.7M weighted. Estimated routing saving 3 to 8 percent, from one measured instance.\nFix: (1) a repair fixer dispatch takes its model from the roster group's fixerModel, not opus by default, and prints the model it chose and the field it read; production and critical-path groups keep opus as their roster field states. (2) a carry-only or delta re-review (review_carry.py answers CARRY) and a record-PR review dispatch on sonnet.\nFailing test first: a fixture roster with fixerModel sonnet; the repair dispatch resolves opus before the change, sonnet after; a record-PR reviewer resolves sonnet.\nMutation proof: ignoring the roster field, or resolving record-PR reviews to opus, turns its arm red.\nNull control: a group with fixerModel opus still dispatches opus, and a first-round non-record review is unchanged.\nCarry: R10-DISPATCH-AT-HANDOFF",
-    "Moved from R10-DISPATCH-AT-HANDOFF carry 11 (0-based) by the carries-open audit (orchestrator s13, 2026-11-09); its own pull request merged and nothing dispatched this carry. Text verbatim:\n\nFrom the s9 orchestrator, 2026-11-01. A dependabot PR cannot be reviewed after a dependabot rebase, and no instrument can repair it. After the rebase, the head carries dev/programme/delivery/<N>.md, but review_packet refuses the dispatch with \"head-note: ## Head does not name the dispatched head\", because dependabot rewrites the body on every rebase. body_patch.sh, the instrument that repairs a body without moving the head, then refuses on \"no commit list was read for this Dependabot pull request (--commits-file)\". It never passes one, so a dependabot body cannot be patched at all. This was hit on #3088 at bffd45a03. Two fixes, either sufficient: body_patch.sh reads the PR's commit list and passes --commits-file for a dependabot PR; or review_packet's head-note check accepts a dependabot PR whose commits are all dependabot's and whose head carries its delivery row. Control: the #3088 shape at bffd45a03 dispatches; a dependabot PR with a non-dependabot commit still refuses. Pairs with the s9-dependabot-row carry here.",

-    "From triage batch 142 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch142.md#P546 at 39a92af760), queue line S14-DISPOSE-WIP-GAP, class-barrier route (9 instances: 2 from queue line BLOCKED-STOP-LANDED and 7 from the s14 handoff-drain seat, stops already landed or superseded that stood owed 9 to 34 h). tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh dispose() accepts only probe/, handoff/, handoff-body/ and review/ refs, so a handoff-wip/ blocked-stop can never be disposed; the carry-fold arm reads no disposition, and tools/audit/seat/bus.sh orphans ignores dispositions too. Fix: dispose() accepts refs/heads/handoff-wip/<topic> at a sha40; the carry-fold arm, the blocked-stop line and bus.sh orphans skip a tip disposed at exactly that sha and re-raise it once the tip moves, as dispose does today for the other families. Self-test arms per reader; null control: an undisposed handoff-wip stop still owes, and a disposed one whose tip moved owes again; a mutant per reader.",
-    "From triage batch 144 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch144.md#P551 at 955fe985ea), queue line S14-BRIEF-OVER-FIVE, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. cloud_dispatch.py dispatch_group renders every carry into a fixer brief with no count check, against fixer.md's 'at most five findings'. Four fixers were published 2026-11-11 00:14-00:16Z with more than five carries and no part named (tick-residue 22, prepr-residue 21, train-residue 14, plan-page-residue 6), and prepr-residue stopped with a question (tick.txt line 115, blocked-stop 87c3ba7353c9): 1 of 4. The orchestrator's answer (re-dispatch 4c83df2f4a) was mechanical: serial parts of at most five carries in index order (0-4, 5-9, 10-14, 15-20, the short remainder folded into the last), each dispatched at the previous part's handoff, part 2's topic <topic>-p2. Fix, fixer role only: without --part, a group with more than five pending carries (roster_lib.pending_carries) is refused and the refusal prints that split; with --part k the brief gains one generated line 'Part k of N: build carries a-b; the others are later parts dispatched at this part's handoff; answer none of them' and the topic is <topic>-p<k> for k over 1 (roster_lib.group_for_branch already maps -pN to its group); five or fewer pending carries render byte-identically. Do not auto-default to part 1 (it would re-dispatch part 1's carries at its own handoff, since they stay pending until its merge), and do not use tools/plan/durations.json carries_per_part (a duration measure, not a scope rule). Self-test: 21 pending carries refuse with the split 0-4, 5-9, 10-14, 15-20; --part 2 renders carries 5-9 and topic -p2; null control: five carries render unchanged with no refusal; a mutant on the count and a mutant on the fold.",
-    "From triage batch 147 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch147.md#P556 at 7a75c6b50c), queue line S14-CARRY-FOLD-WRONG-CHECKOUT, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. cloud_relay.sh carry_fold_arm already fetches refs/heads/handoff/<topic>, adds a detached worktree at FETCH_HEAD and runs body_push.sh inside it (since 67a823c2e7, 2026-11-08), yet the fold still ran from a worktree at origin/main's tip: 'body_push: handoff/r10-plan-page-residue is at 428aa56b7cdc, this checkout at d08ed31049cd' (tick.txt.relay.log, 2026-11-11 about 01:06Z) and 18 times for r10-process-slim-p2 (tick-loop.log, 2026-11-10 08:36-19:18Z); the source counts four handoffs needing a hand seat. The same two steps run sequentially in a seat worktree land the right head (428aa56b7cdc), so the cause is not the arm's logic alone; candidate, unproven: FETCH_HEAD overwritten in the relay's checkout between fetch and add. Fix: (1) reproduce first: log the fold worktree's head on the failing pass; (2) fetch into a private ref (+refs/heads/handoff/<topic>:refs/hpo-relay/fold/<topic>) and add the worktree at that ref, never FETCH_HEAD; (3) before body_push, assert the worktree HEAD equals the ls-remote tip, else print 'OWED carry-fold <topic> worktree at <a>, handoff at <b>'. -    "From triage batch 149 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch149.md#P559 at 7696a3ba07), queue line S14-BRIEF-CARRIES-MOVING-TARGET, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. review_packet.py handoff() (lines 646-671 at d08ed31049) counts the group's roster carries at the live --roster-ref, so carries appended after a fixer's dispatch refuse its finished body: R10-TICK-RESIDUE grew 21 (2cd3f772f4, at dispatch; its dispatch PROMPT ea7df0fc2e holds Carry 0..20) to 27 (2f484ff60e), and the bodies seat's body_push refused 'carry 21'..'carry 26' then, two minutes later, 'carry 27'. +    "From triage batch 144 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch144.md#P551 at 955fe985ea), queue line S14-BRIEF-OVER-FIVE, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. cloud_dispatch.py dispatch_group renders every carry into a fixer brief with no count check, against fixer.md's 'at most five findings'. Four fixers were published 2026-11-11 00:14-00:16Z with more than five carries and no part named (tick-residue 22, prepr-residue 21, train-residue 14, plan-page-residue 6), and prepr-residue stopped with a question (tick.txt line 115, blocked-stop 87c3ba7353c9): 1 of 4. The orchestrator's answer (re-dispatch 4c83df2f4a) was mechanical: serial parts of at most five carries in index order (0-4, 5-9, 10-14, 15-20, the short remainder folded into the last), each dispatched at the previous part's handoff, part 2's topic <topic>-p2. Fix, fixer role only: without --part, a group with more than five pending carries (roster_lib.pending_carries) is refused and the refusal prints that split; with --part k the brief gains one generated line 'Part k of N: build carries a-b; the others are later parts dispatched at this part's handoff; answer none of them' and the topic is <topic>-p<k> for k over 1 (roster_lib.group_for_branch already maps -pN to its group); five or fewer pending carries render byte-identically. Do not auto-default to part 1 (it would re-dispatch part 1's carries at its own handoff, since they stay pending until its merge), and do not use tools/plan/durations.json carries_per_part (a duration measure, not a scope rule). Self-test: 21 pending carries refuse with the split 0-4, 5-9, 10-14, 15-20; --part 2 renders carries 5-9 and topic -p2; null control: five carries render unchanged with no refusal; a mutant on the count and a mutant on the fold. [implemented: a7887485de]",

     "From triage batch 155 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch155.md#P566 at 1a3e60ac78), queue line S14-RELAY-CADENCE, option (c), a repair of an observed wrong behaviour. Build it with this group's P546 carry (handoff-wip dispose), which changes the same arm. tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh carry_fold_arm writes cloud-folded only on success (line 843) and checks it by \"<topic> <bsha>\" (line 821), where bsha is the tip of handoff-wip/<topic>-body. On each failure, body_push.sh keeps the refused body as a new parentless commit (body_push.sh line 106). So r10-train-residue's fold re-ran body_push (at least 60 s each) on every relay pass and pushed a new kept commit each time: d982cc00b7, 2cfd2407be, ef11984257 and 7d1f0419ad between 01:20Z and 02:17Z, all with tree 6ae9f4ff2db8 and BODY.md blob 80dc1f17dc70. Meanwhile its body had already reached PR 3424 (handoff-body/r10-train-residue 2fa8399213, BODIED 01:44:40Z). A memo keyed on the kept commit would never hit. Fix: a failed fold or body_push records \"<topic> <stop sha> <BODY.md blob sha> <handoff/<topic> tip> <refusal last line>\" in <state>/cloud-fold-failed. A pass skips a stop whose stop sha, BODY.md blob and handoff/<topic> tip all equal a record, and prints \"cloud_relay: OWED carry-fold <topic> unchanged since <utc>: <line>\" so the level still owes it. A change to any of the three re-runs it as today. Self-test: the train-residue shape (a new kept commit with the same blob, the same stop and code tip) is skipped; a changed blob, or a moved handoff tip, re-runs; null control: a stop with no record runs as today; a mutant keyed on the kept commit sha fails the first case.",
     "From triage batch 156 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch156.md#P568 at 23c4d90aa2), queue line S14-PACKET-REFUSES-DEFERRAL-RED, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour (a class: 14 dispatch outputs hold the refusal on gate; today PRs 3424, 3404, 3429). tools/audit/seat/review_packet.py main gathers read = required_reading(a.pr, head) whenever a required context is red (line 602 at 63bcdcaad8), which is merge_train.py classify --pr, the answer bus.sh dispatch uses. build turns a WAIT or GREEN read into a required-wait or required-green advisory for the required-red arm (lines 544-557, #3379). But the red-unanswered arm (lines 542-543) refuses the same gate red from unanswered_reds(..., gate[2], ...) without consulting the read. Reproduced at about 02:50Z: classify 561edf4c8f --pr 3404 and classify 498c2ab042 --pr 3429 both read \"WAIT ... pending: gate red: none\" beside completed-failure gate check-runs of edit runs (42945740958; 42946500173 and 42946386595), and bus3404.out and bus3429.out both refused \"red-unanswered: concluded red check `gate` is not named in ## Red checks\". Fix: in build, an entry of facts[\"red_unanswered\"] that is in REQUIRED gets no red-unanswered refusal when facts[\"required_read\"]'s word is WAIT or GREEN; its required-wait or required-green advisory already names it. A RED, APPROVAL-OWED or unreadable read, and any red outside REQUIRED, keep today's refusal. Do not add a second classify call (Train.classify_at or otherwise): the read at line 602 decides, and a second read costs API calls and could disagree with the first. A local version under s14/tools-wt-63bcdcaa calls classify_at again; build the permanent form on the existing read. Self-test: red_unanswered=[\"gate\"] with required_read (\"WAIT\", \"pending: gate red: none\") renders one required-wait advisory and no refusal; the same with (\"RED\", ...) refuses red-unanswered and required-red; null control: a non-required red (\"tests / fast\") with a WAIT read still refuses red-unanswered; a mutant dropping the WAIT/GREEN condition fails the first case. [dismissed: superseded by the corrected P568 carry (batch 156): the local fix 572755fb70 reads the gathered required_read, not a second classify]",

     "From triage batch 162 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch162.md#P575 at 98099a02a3), queue line DISPOSE-NEVER-CLEARS-BOARD, arm (a2), a repair of an observed wrong behaviour; build it with this group's P546 carry (handoff-wip dispose and orphans honouring dispositions), which changes the same orphan arm. This carry adds tools/audit/seat/ledger.py to the group's files (no open group owns it). Cause: ledger.py covered() (line 393 at main dbd1ab5b07) returns False when the merge-base..tip diff is empty (\"if rc or not names.strip(): return False\"), and reverse_applies has no patch, so landed() (line 464) returns None for a tip whose net change is nothing; bus.sh orphans and the relay's level both classify through landed(). Instance: handoff/r10-body-contract-p3-bootstrap cdad1183b2, 0 files in its three-dot diff against main, 3 '+' commits in git cherry (an add-then-remove pair), STRANDED for 30 h and disposed 5 times. Fix: an empty merge-base..tip diff read with rc 0 lands, as its own named reason (for example \"empty\") ahead of \"content\"; rc != 0 stays not-landed. Self-test arms: the add-then-remove net-empty shape lands as empty; mutant dropping the branch makes it STRANDED again; null control: a tip with one real uncovered line stays None. Do not add a \"handoff or PR head newer than BLOCKED\" rule for blocked stops (REJECTED in the ruling: 0 of the 3 named stops would clear).",
     "From triage batch 162 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch162.md#P576 at 98099a02a3), queue line PENDING-CI-RED-DEADLOCK, a repair of an observed wrong behaviour (PR 3419, relay overridden once with HPO_RELAY_CLASSIFY; PR 3425 at c6becb31 next). Cause: tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh's pending-ci block (lines 259-275 at main dbd1ab5b07) dies on a moved head (line 268) before any carry test, while line 405 already relays a verdict across a range review_carry.py answers CARRY; a red the branch did not cause and only a main merge cures is refused at line 273 before the cure and at line 268 after it. Fix: at line 268, before refusing a moved head, run ${HPO_RELAY_CARRY:-python3 -I review_carry.py} <drafted head> <live head> --verdict-only; on CARRY classify the LIVE head and post on GREEN, saying in the relay line that the verdict was carried; on FRESH-REVIEW or CANNOT DECIDE keep today's refusal and re-dispatch; RED at the live head still refuses. Self-test (pending-ci arms near lines 1183-1190, reusing the carry-ok/no/un stubs at 1161-1163): moved head + carry-ok + GREEN posts; moved head + carry-no refuses; null control same head + GREEN posts as today; mutant skipping the carry call makes the carry-ok arm refuse.",
-    "From triage batch 166 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch166.md#P580 at ef2659ab89), queue line CARRY-FOLD-ARM-REJUDGES-EVERY-PASS, a cost repair measured at about 30% of the relay pass. Cause: tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh carry_fold_arm (lines 805-850 at main a7628041dc) fetches and shows BLOCKED.md for every handoff-wip/<topic> ref on every pass (line 818; 27-33 s for 46 topics on 2026-11-11), and records only a successful fold in cloud-folded (line 843), so a fold whose body_push.sh fails after carry_fold.py succeeded re-runs carry_fold.py and body_push.sh every pass (2 topics, about 181 s a pass). Fix: a judged-outcome memo, state file cloud-fold-judged, one line per topic: <topic> <wip sha> <body sha> <outcome> <epoch>. (1) Take both shas from the ls-remote the arm already makes; a topic whose (wip sha, body sha) is in the memo is neither fetched nor shown. (2) Re-write its stored OWED level line into cloud-level-fold.$$, because level_set (line 849) replaces the whole carry-fold level each pass and a dropped line clears an owed item silently. (3) A failure after the fold (carry_fold rc != 0 or body_push rc != 0) is retried only once its memo entry is 3600 s old, because an ancestry red can clear with no ref moving. (4) Success still appends to cloud-folded. Self-test arms beside the mut:foldonly cases (lines 1827-1850): an unchanged nothing-folded ref is not refetched AND its OWED level line still stands (the null control: a memo that dropped the line must fail it); a moved wip or body ref is judged again; a failed body push is retried after the TTL and not before. Do not widen dispose to handoff-wip/ (REJECTED in the same ruling).",

     "From triage batch 170 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch170.md#P585 at 38f52d84c6), queue line PENDING-CI-RED-TERMINAL. This repairs an observed wrong behaviour (PRs 3425 and 3430, 2026-11-11). Build it with this group's P576 carry, which changes the same arm.\n\nCause: tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh's pending-ci block refuses finally on classify RED (line 273 at main a7628041dc). die returns rc 2, so line 371 writes the pair to cloud-relayed.refused, and line 343 then skips that pair forever. Both RED refusals in the tick-loop log were reds that cleared with no fixer work:\n- PR 3425 at c6becb3175: pr-contract died on main's carry_audit.py calling roster_lib.carry_receivable, which the branch's base lacked. The fixer's main merge c93903f075 cured it, with review_carry CARRY and gate green.\n- PR 3430 at ba0ed526cc: gate printed \"run(s) ... at this head are still being judged\" and went green at the same head 49 min later.\nNeither draft was ever re-read, and nothing re-dispatched either PR. P576 as written keeps \"RED at the live head still refuses\", so it cures neither.\n\nFix:\n- On RED, hold the draft as WAIT does (return 75). Print one line per pass: \"cloud_relay: HELD-PENDING-CI-RED #<pr> <head>: <red contexts> (classify RED)\". Only open_pr.sh runs `bus.sh rekey`, so a reviewer dispatched at the handoff after the PR already exists is never keyed to it, and cloud_relay.sh topic_verdict reports HELD-NOPR forever. On 2026-11-11 that was 14 of 42 handoff dispatches: 8 were re-keyed by hand and 6 were held (relay level handoff-verdict-held; #3451 about 1.7 h, #3462 about 4 h). Repair: after it records, dispatch_handoff re-keys the new record itself whenever the PR is known. That is (a) a `<topic> * <pr>` line already in <bus>/rekeyed, a local read, or (b) an explicit `--pr N`, which the dispatcher passes when it dispatches at a handoff for an open PR. Do not key only on fix/<topic>[-pr]: #3457's branch is fix/supply-stale-step-write. No API call per relay pass. Demonstration: the bus.sh selftest asserts that a second dispatch-handoff for a topic already re-keyed to #55 adds a `55 <head> ...` record and a rekeyed line, and that `--pr 56` keys a fresh topic. -    "Moved from R10-FLY-STANDING-CONTRACTS carry 19 (0-based) by the carries-open audit (orchestrator s13, 2026-11-09); its own pull request merged and nothing dispatched this carry. Text verbatim:\n\nOwner order (tvofi, 2026-11-01, verbatim: \"Make sure judging and folding every useful standing loop into the orchestrator machinery on main is planned in the roster, including describing the hooks and a script to set them up if they are not there\"). Widens STEP 1 and STEP 2 of this group's brief; the design (design/R10-FLY-STANDING-CONTRACTS) carries it.\n\n(1) JUDGE EVERY LOOP. The live-usage review gets one row per out-of-tree loop the round ran, with a verdict of fold (into a beat job in the job table, or into the tracked instrument it wraps), keep-as-is (already tracked; name the file), or drop (with the reason it bought nothing). Fold only what is useful, measured from its log: what it caught that nothing else caught. The loops the s9 orchestrator ran on 2026-11-01, by role (ask the orchestrator for their paths; cite none in tracked text):\n- the sweep runner (roster drain, pulse, cancel sweep, opener-log forward, disk floor, plan beat, owed_unactioned) \u2014 already STEP 2;\n- the roster drain \u2014 already STEP 2;\n- the opener driver, which acts on the tick's handoff-no-pr and handoff-update lines. Fold its 2026-11-01 retry semantics: mark a (topic, sha) done only on success; retry a failure after a backoff (30 min), at most three times; then print one STUCK line the digest carries. Before that change it marked a handoff done at start, and 7 handoffs stood stranded 1-16 h on 2026-11-01. It is the opener carry b94-P337 builds, plus these semantics;\n- the standing head watcher: one line per open-PR head move to the event digest (a reviewer or relay may be owed). A one-shot watcher's exit was lost across a compaction and a reviewer went unowed;\n- the orchestrator wake loop: exits, and so wakes the orchestrator, on a new sweep digest, a new event line or a new STUCK line. After a compaction nobody re-armed it, and owed lines stood unread for up to 15.8 h while every instrument had flagged them;\n- the triage seat's pass loop: releases held roster lines once the batch they wait on is on main, reads back landings, and exits on a new proposal or V-entry;\n- the merge seat's watch loop: polls main and the frozen hotfix heads, and touches the merge seat's alive file;\n- the dispatch head-await loop, now the standing head watcher above;\n- tick_loop.sh, cloud_relay.sh and state_docs.py beat \u2014 tracked already; verdict keep-as-is unless the review finds a gap.\nThe design names the digest and event files under $HPO_STATE_DIR that one wake reads, so no loop writes only to a private log (the 2026-11-01 class).\n\n(2) HOOKS, DESCRIBED AND INSTALLABLE. Owner of the standing-seat identity and liveness seam (pulse.py, sweep_template.md).\n\nOriginal text, verbatim:\nFrom triage P236 (V150, orchestrator s10, 2026-10-28): extend this group's single-instance lock to the orchestrator session itself. On resume, before its first dispatch, the session takes a lock record holding its own long-lived pid (the session binary, never the calling shell's $PPID, which dies with the call) and its start time. A second session that finds the lock held by a live pid whose command is the session binary refuses to dispatch, logs both pids, and stops; a holder pid that is gone, or names another command, is stale and is taken over. Liveness is read from the pid (`ps -p <pid> -o command=`), never from stop lines, because a reboot leaves none. The in-tree resume steps (`tools/audit/seat/handover_prompt.py` \"Resume steps\", `tools/audit/seat/sweep_template.md`) name the step; `handover_prompt.py` is not in this group's files (edit-brief is the roster seat's call). Self-test: a live other holder refuses; null controls: a dead holder pid and a reused pid naming another command are both taken over, and the session's own pid is not a duplicate. Mutation proof: dropping the command match makes the reused-pid null control refuse. Basis: after the 2026-10-28 ~07:07Z reboot, sessions 10472 (08:16Z) and 21371 (08:19Z) resumed one round; 10472 dispatched seats at ~08:38Z; it was killed on the owner's instruction and nine seats were relaunched at 08:44:41Z. triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch48.md#P236\n\nCarry: R10-FLY-SEAT-ALIVE-ID",

Text verbatim:\n\nPart 3: add a `loop-cwd` row to `beat_jobs.py` and `beatd.jobs.json`, every 900 s, for the long-lived loops outside the job table, named by a new overlay key of `pgrep -f` patterns (a lacking key prints `UNREADABLE`, as every row does). For each pid: read `lsof -a -p <pid> -d cwd -Fin`, then `stat` the named path. An inode that differs, or a path that does not resolve, writes `DEAD <name> pid=<p> cwd gone` to the digest. An `lsof` that returns nothing writes `UNREADABLE`, never DEAD. Compare inodes, never only the path: `lsof` keeps printing the old path, and a recreated checkout resolves (four loops on 2026-11-04 at 18:37:06Z). A process absent from `pgrep` is not a line of this row. Fixture arms with a real child process: (a) directory removed writes DEAD; (b) directory recreated at the same path writes DEAD; (c) directory live writes nothing (the null control). Mutant: comparing only that the path exists fails (b). triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch119.md#P383 at 8439f738e",

     "From triage batch 167 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch167.md#P581 at b2247e2528), queue line BEAT-CHECKOUT-ROTATION-BY-HAND, measured at 8 of 25 opener attempts (32%) refused STALE CHECKOUT (tools/audit/seat/pr_args.sh:188 at main a7628041dc) in open-driver.log 2026-11-10T09:50Z..2026-11-11T06:12Z, with measured open delays of 36 and 48 min, each cleared only by a hand rotation of the beat's tools checkout. Fix: beatd restarts itself in place, the shape of tick_loop.sh fresh_check (lines 95-112) as extended by s6-batch145.md#P552. Between passes, when its own checkout is behind origin/main on tools/audit/seat or tools/pr (a path-filtered count; beatd.py:488-496's STALE-CODE counts every commit and stays the alarm), with a clean tree, no commit outside origin/main (git rev-list origin/main..HEAD empty), no job instance running, and main's beatd.py compiling, it runs git checkout -q -B <its branch> origin/main and re-execs itself with a once-per-start guard. On any refusal it prints the why. A refusal that persists into a second pass prints a BEAT-STALE line the board can key on. The checkout path does not change, so beatd.tools, orch-checkout and the launch files are not rewritten; no separate rotate instrument (REJECTED in the same ruling). Self-test arms: behind on the paths and idle restarts; behind only off the paths does not (null control); a running instance defers to the next pass; a dirty tree or a local commit refuses with its why; a broken main beatd.py refuses.      "TRAIN-WAIT-UNSTALE (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch219.md#P671 at 36234d2447), line numbers at 61afd4f2db. tools/audit/seat/merge_train.py `unstale` (:3648-3672) runs only from `land` (:2722), after the head wait. `wait_ci` (:1905 on) reads a queued check-run over a completed run as pending on every poll, so the wait never reaches `unstale` and ends only at its cap. Instance (2026-11-11): #3455 at cbf80c6e waited from poll 1 to poll 19 (t+4370 s of 7320 s) on gate check-run 130782293389, queued over completed run 43089450993 (left over from the outage), while four newer gate runs at the head were green. A hand `merge_train.py unstale cbf80c6e` cleared it in one call. That was about 73 min of stalled critical-path train with 21 PRs spooled. Fix: split the re-run half of `unstale` into a step that returns the runs it re-ran, each run once per head; `unstale` keeps that step plus its wait. `wait_ci` calls the step at poll 3 and every 6th poll after, while a required context is pending, logs the STALE line and keeps waiting, with no recursive `wait_ci`. Cost: one actions/runs/<id> read per pending required check-run, at those polls only. Self-test: the #3455 shape is re-run once by poll 3 and the wait then concludes. Null control: a check-run pending over a run that is still in progress is not re-run. A second pass at the same head re-runs nothing. Mutant: dropping the call keeps the fixture waiting to its cap. [moved: R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK-FINAL]",
     "R10-TRAIN-RESIDUE-P2 (triage: dev/programme/triage/s6-batch228.md#P690 at ab46cc3050), line numbers at ef579d9ba6. tools/audit/seat/merge_train.py already_held (:1278) answers `skipped by this train at this same verdict` (:1302) for an entry the train skipped even when its live head has since moved and the verdict carries to it. On 2026-11-12 #3454 (skipped on A5: head moved by the merge-main bot, review_carry CARRY) and #3460 (skipped on a head red cured by a clean main merge, CARRY), both outside-derived, were refused re-admission and each needed a direct merge by hand under the owner's deadlock exception. Fix: already_held does not hold a skipped entry at the same verdict when the live head differs from the skipped head and the verdict carries to the live head (the train's own review_carry); otherwise it holds as today. Self-test: both shapes (A5 head move; red cured by a main merge) re-admitted, failing on the base and passing on the fix; null controls: same head stays held; a moved head whose verdict does not carry stays held. [moved: R10-ROUND-PLAYBOOK-FINAL]"

    "brief": "Hotfix for a live defect on the released v7.1.11, reported by the owner (tvofi, 2026-11-11 about 21:27Z, verbatim: \"Live bug on v7.1.11. All text in the \"Health\" Card tab is missing\"). It is outside the open-PR cap, at outside-class priority, by the orchestrator's ruling (s14, 2026-11-11). It was not a cost/benefit question.\n\nObserved, from the owner's screenshot (own Home Assistant install, dark theme, card dialog \"Heat pump plan\", Health tab selected, header chip \"1 input stale\"):\n(a) Input tiles, 4 + 3 visible: each shows only its status chip (\"Fresh\", green dot). The input's name, and any value or age text above the chip, are absent.\n(b) The heading \"What the model has learned\" renders. Its five rows show only the value and bar (\"197 W/K\", \"Window scale 1.00\", \"99 % of the settings' estimate\", \"0.7 \u00b0C per hour\", and one row that is only an em dash). Every row label and explanatory sub-text is absent.\n(c) The \"Download diagnostics\" button renders with its text. One empty row or panel follows it.\n(d) The heading \"First-plan checklist\" renders. Its three rows show only the green tick, and every item text is absent.\nRows keep their height. Headings, values, chips and button text render, and every per-row label or description string is blank.\n(e) The header says one input is stale, but every visible tile reads \"Fresh\". The view is scrolled, so an eighth tile may sit above the fold. Check whether the stale input is shown at all.\nNot known: light theme, v7.1.10, a fresh install, locale or language, browser, and whether other tabs are hit. v7.1.11 was stamped 2026-11-11T16:08Z. Product merges in v7.1.10..v7.1.11 include #3405 and #3433 (water set-point feedback), #3398 (combi tank; its branch merged the de catalogue and SHIPPED_LANGS into the card) and #3447 (diagnostics location redaction). These are leads only, not a cause.\n\nWork, in order:\n1. Reproduce first, with a failing card test through the browser rig (tests/card_browser.mjs and tests/card_rig.mjs) or tests/card.mjs. It renders the Health tab from a hass state that has inputs, learned-model rows and checklist rows, and asserts that each row's label text is non-empty. It also asserts that a stale input renders as stale, not \"Fresh\". It runs in every shipped language.\n2. Find the change, in v7.1.10..v7.1.11 or earlier, that blanked the labels. Say whether other tabs or languages are affected.\n3. Fix at the owning seam (the catalogue lookup, the key spelling or the render path that drops the string), not by patching strings in place.\n4. Mutation proof: reverting the fix turns the new test red. Null control: a tab not affected stays green.\n5. The body says why the existing card tests, browser tests and screenshots were green while every Health label was blank.\nRoot cause is recorded beside this fix by R10-RCA-CARD-HEALTH-TEXT, never inside it.",

    "brief": "HOTFIX. Since #3475 (merge d655c4a8) tools/audit/seat/mutant_run.py reads /proc, which macOS lacks: os.listdir of /proc in family() (near line 134) and in _reap() (near line 158) raise FileNotFoundError, every run exits 1, and the prepr self-test fails five checks, which blocks every full-prepr open and update on the macOS host. Fix: failing test first, a self-test case that runs family() and _reap() with /proc absent (for example a patched listdir raising FileNotFoundError), red at main and green after. When /proc is absent build the process table from ps -axo pid=,ppid=,pgid=,stat= with the session from os.getsid, and _reap() skips. The four fork-cap self-test cases that need a subreaper or another process environment (chain leaves its session / cleans its env at every link) are skipped on a host without /proc WITH a printed line naming each skipped case and why, never silently. mutant_run.py --self-test and the three mutants_line fixtures in tools/pr/prepr.sh (around lines 2388-2412) must pass on Linux and with /proc absent. Update the instrument doc line in tools/audit/seat/instruments/mutant_run.py.md. Candidate patch, a starting point you must verify and not trust:\ndiff --git a/tools/audit/seat/mutant_run.py b/tools/audit/seat/mutant_run.py\nindex f47dac0b3d..b63be00d77 100644\n--- a/tools/audit/seat/mutant_run.py\n+++ b/tools/audit/seat/mutant_run.py\n@@ -131,6 +131,25 @@ def family(sid: int, token: str, skip: frozenset = frozenset(), root: int = 0) -\n     had before the command). The ppid leg is the one a descendant cannot leave: setsid, setpgid,\n     a cleared environment and an exited parent all keep it under `root`. Zombies are not counted.\"\"\"\n     me, mark, kids, out = os.getpid(), (\"MUTANT_RUN_TOKEN=\" + token).encode(), {}, set()\n+    if not os.path.isdir(\"/proc\"):  # macOS/BSD: no procfs; the ps table carries ppid/pgid, os.getsid the session\n+        rows = subprocess.run([\"ps\", \"-axo\", \"pid=,ppid=,pgid=,stat=\"], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout\n+        for line in rows.splitlines():\n+            try:\n+                pid, ppid, pgrp, state = line.split()[:4]\n+                if int(pid) == me or state.startswith(\"Z\"):\n+                    continue\n+                kids.setdefault(int(ppid), []).append(int(pid))\n+                if sid and (sid == int(pgrp) or sid == os.getsid(int(pid))):\n+                    out.add(int(pid))\n+            except (OSError, ValueError):\n+                continue\n+        todo, seen = ([c for c in kids.get(root, []) if c not in skip] if root else []), set()\n+        while todo:\n+            pid = todo.pop()\n+            if pid not in seen:\n+                seen.add(pid)\n+                todo.extend(kids.get(pid, []))\n+        return sorted(out | seen)\n     for d in os.listdir(\"/proc\"):\n         if not d.isdigit() or int(d) == me:\n             continue\n@@ -156,7 +175,7 @@ def family(sid: int, token: str, skip: frozenset = frozenset(), root: int = 0) -\n def _reap(skip: frozenset, keep: int) -> None:\n     \"\"\"Collect the exit status of every orphan reparented to this subreaper (children not in\n     `skip` and not the command `keep`, which Popen waits for), so dead ones leave no zombie.\"\"\"\n-    for d in os.listdir(\"/proc\"):\n+    for d in (os.listdir(\"/proc\") if os.path.isdir(\"/proc\") else []):\n         try:\n             pid = int(d)\n             stat = Path(\"/proc\", d, \"stat\").read_text()\n\nPR body answers the red-check trigger: the cheaper detector is a no-/proc self-test case in CI.     "architect_note": "",
    "brief": "HOTFIX. Defect: open_pr.sh closes a freshly opened pull request when its row push is refused by a host-environment self-test red. The p2 push runs the full pre-PR check when the body carries no Prepr receipt, that run includes the macOS self-test, and the self-test has failed on the orchestrator host since PR 3475. On any non-zero exit the EXIT trap runs op_close_unrowed, which calls gh pr close whenever origin lacks the row commit; nothing classifies the refusal. Measured evidence: PR 3508 (the plan binding chain) was closed this way on 2026-11-12; PRs 3511, 3512 and 3513 stayed open only because the orchestrator killed the openers before the trap ran. The opener cap is held at zero on this host meanwhile.\n\nFix: when every REFUSE row in the p2 log is the pre-PR self-test step failing while every other step passes, op_close_unrowed leaves the pull request OPEN, prints one line saying the row is owed (the existing ROWLESS path that update_pr.sh ensure_row already serves, naming update_pr.sh with the branch as the owed row), sets PR_ATTEMPT to opened with the PR number so the driver neither retries nor closes, and exits non-zero. Any other refusal (body contract, figure blocks, conflict, stop signal) keeps today close. Guard: if the pull request own diff touches the self-tested script, the refusal is the branch own and it still closes. Out of scope: whether p2 should fall back to the full pre-PR check at all (owned by the opener reuse group).\n\nSelf-test arm in the open_pr.sh self-test (through pr_args.sh): a faked p2 log whose only REFUSE row is the self-test step must leave the PR open with exactly one ROWLESS/OWED line and a non-zero exit. Null control: a faked p2 log with a body-contract REFUSE row still closes the PR, and a mixed log (self-test plus another REFUSE) still closes. Red-then-green demonstration: write the arm first and show it red on origin/main (the PR is closed), then green with the fix; mutate the classifier to always-leave-open and show the null-control arm kill it. Report the structure.py budgets row for tools/audit/seat. Update the open_pr entry in INSTRUMENTS.md.\n\nTriage: P742 ACCEPT round 10 (batch 246)",

The P638 reopening condition holds: the redispatch-loop hotfix is done and a green verdicted entry was still skipped.\n\nFix: read every page of the contexts under the gh_read rule, so a head with more than one page of check-runs is classified on all of them. Start only after R10-HOTFIX-SELFTEST-SCOPE-RERECORD has merged; base on that main. Cause being closed: the measured table tools/ci/selftest_closures.json is repaired by hand re-records, and the record subcommand of tools/ci/selftest_scope.py replaces a step's entry with one recording (at origin/main the record function ends by starting from the old steps only under --step, else from an empty dict, then updating with the new measurement), so a re-record taken at a stale base drops reads main measures. Merges #3409, #3419 and #3506 dropped 660, 8 and 10 entries; 6 of the 20 drift refusals at f47250150a were such drops. Two changes. (1) record unions: in both the per-step and the whole-table mode it keeps every read the committed entry holds and adds the new measurement; removing a read becomes a separate, explicit operation the operator must name. Over-approximation is already documented as safe in the tool's header. (2) A table-shrink guard: when a diff changes the table, refuse every files, probes, dirs or git item, and every index or objects flag, that the head's entry drops relative to the base's entry for the same step, unless the same diff deletes that path; a step removed from the workflow is not a drop. Put the guard in the existing static subcommand, which governance.yml already runs in the scope's own self-test step on every pull request that touches the table (the table is in GATE_FILES, so that run is FULL); do not edit any workflow and do not add a job step. On a push to main compare against the first parent. Because the guard reads the base's table, the self-test step's measured reads change: re-record that step on Linux with strace, by the new union record, in this same pull request, and show the drift check clean for it. Tests: a failing test first for each change (recording one step over an entry holding a read the new trace lacks keeps that read; a fixture diff that drops an entry is refused and one that deletes the path is not). Mutants in tools/ci/selftest_scope_mutants.sh, each killed: restoring the replace semantics; removing the deletion exemption. Demonstration the body must show: the guard over every first-parent merge that touched the table from ba6fd7e8b0^ to f47250150a refuses exactly #3409, #3419 and #3506 and passes the other 12, including #3507 (its deletion is exempt); that is the null control, it must neither fire on additive merges nor skip. Then list its result on every later table-touching merge up to the base, the RERECORD merge included, and explain any it refuses. Add one class for this mechanism to dev/audit/config/bugclasses.json naming the RCA, so tools/audit/fold_ledger.py check tracks it; part A (main re-records mechanically) is ruled to round 11 and is not this group. No budget raise; re-check the ratchets. Cutoff: if this group has no pull request in review by 2026-11-14T18:00Z the orchestrator moves it to round 11; do not let it hold the round-10 closure.",

+    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item 4f264267: carry-201 entry 0 (file removed): the round-5 D11-02 finding (#1301), whose fix is rule \"rows\" in tools/release/stamp.py -- the stamp now re-applies `record`'s own predicate to the window it is about to close -- Stamp-time precondition, not an opportunity: `tools/release/stamp.py` REFUSES to stamp when any merged pull request in the window it would close has no disposition row, and the only override is the explicit `--allow-rowless` flag, which prints the instrument's own output before proceeding. +    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item 5cf71ed3: carry-201 entry 1 (file removed): the round-5 D13-01 finding (#1303), whose fix makes the CFR exclusion list a registered in-tree artifact that the instrument reads from the repository root, failing closed when it is absent -- Constraint on how the exclusion list is carried: it is ONE registered artifact at `dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json`, a top-level object with an `excluded_jobs` map keyed by check-run name beside a `_comment`, in the shape dev/governance/config/policy_budgets.json takes. Carry: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE (items 5cf71ed3)",
+    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item a39359c9: carry-201 entry 2 (file removed): the round-6 D11-02 finding (#1403), whose fix adds the `Restore the check source from the base commit` step to the `policy-docs` job of .github/workflows/governance.yml and the entry-point exit-status arm to tools/policy/policy_lint_mutants.mjs -- Constraint on any seat adding a corpus check or moving a step in the `policy-docs` job of .github/workflows/governance.yml. (1) A corpus check is a function inside tools/policy/policy_lint.mjs, named in that file's own lists of check names, never a module beside it: the new step restores the `.mjs` and `.py` programs under tools/policy/ plus tools/policy/vendor from the base commit, and a file the base does not carry cannot be restored, so a checker added as its own module stays the pull request's own and the corpus is graded by the copy under test -- the defect of #1403 in its original shape. (2) The job now has two halves and that step is the boundary. The five steps above it -- tools/policy/policy_lint_mutants.mjs, the `--self-test` runs of tools/policy/fragments_sync.mjs and tools/policy/record-predicate/sweep.mjs, the `--hooks` run of tools/policy/policy_lint.mjs and the `self-test` run of tools/pr/gh_comment.py -- drive the PULL REQUEST's programs on purpose, because a self-test that ran the base's copy would report on neither; every step below it grades the pull request's data with the base's programs. Moving a step across that boundary silently changes which side of it a check is on. (3) The pin covers programs, not criteria: dev/governance/config/policy_budgets.json, dev/governance/config/policy_known_bad.json, dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json and the sync surface tools/policy/fragments_sync.mjs checks against .claude/workflows/web-fragments.md stay the pull request's, because a fixture's expected outcome has to be the data under test. Those three places -- a criteria file, a checker outside the restored paths, and the self-test block above the step -- are where the pull request's own copy is still what runs, and that is where a later round hunting this class of defect should look. Carry: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE (items a39359c9)",
+    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item ad65f1ff: carry-201 entry 3 (file removed): the pull request fixing the round-6 D13-03 finding (#1407), which makes the round-4 DORA instrument (dev/audit/rounds/round4/D11/dora_keys.py, `cfr_keyings`) report the change-failure rate at BOTH keyings -- at the merge commit, where `dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json` acts, and at the pull-request head, where a merge is gated -- and pins the pair in tests/entities.py -- Constraint on every change-failure rate a later round reports: the rate is TWO numbers -- the merge-keyed rate and the pull-request-head-keyed rate -- printed together and never one alone, because `dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json` narrows only the merge keying while a merge is gated on the head keying. The artifact's `_keying` field states that rule for whoever edits the list, and dev/governance/dimensions/D13.md's DORA item asks for the list without naming a keying, which is how the single-number report arose. A check-run name that fails at the pull-request head is a real gate red: it must NOT be added to the list, however much it costs the merge keying, and a report that names such a name as excluded is the escape this carry closes. +    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item a76b2d16: carry-201 entry 4 (file removed): the pull request fixing the round-7 D13-06 finding (#1476), which makes the same round-4 DORA instrument report the rate PER CHECK-RUN NAME at both keyings (dev/audit/rounds/round4/D11/dora_keys.py, `cfr_by_name`), and records `mutation` in dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json's new `not_excluded` map as a name deliberately NOT on the list -- Constraint on how a later round reports the change-failure rate: per-name at both keyings, BESIDE the two aggregates and never instead of them. Two aggregate rates hide which name separates the surfaces, and here the whole difference is one name -- and the instrument cannot decide whether that name's merge-surface red is a change failure at all. `mutation` fails 4 of the window's 31 merge commits (two pairs merged 4-5 s apart, i.e. two batch merges) where neither pull request's own head failed it, because the lane's inventory is the TREE (`tests/mutation_table.py`'s `unpinned_sites`) and on a push to `main` its scoping base IS `HEAD`, so a batch's merged tree can red it where no head did. Nothing in the finding says the red is spurious; it says whether a merge-surface red on a batch-merged tree is a change failure is the exclusion list owner's judgement, and it is recorded rather than made: `mutation` stays off the list, and dev/governance/config/cfr_exclusions.json's `not_excluded` map carries the reason and the citation so the next reader meets the answer in the artifact rather than in a merged pull request's comments. +    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item d245b092: carry-201 entry 5 (file removed): the v6.7.8 stamp hotfix (the pull request repairing main's red entities check after the stamp commit), whose fix restores the may-drift entry R9-F2.3 (#1723) suspended for its wood_coil claim -- which the stamp deleted without restoring -- and moves the R8-P3 coverage ruling into tests/env_drift.py's may_drift_coverage_error, executed by --claims-only -- Stamp-time precondition, not an opportunity: a stamp that empties the claim list owes every suspended may-drift entry back, verbatim, because a name may leave the may-drift list only while THIS release claims it (the R8-P3 ruling, option (a)) and the claim the suspension was made for expires with the stamp. +    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item 0112100b: carry-201 entry 9 (file removed): the pull request for R9-RC-BLAS-KERNEL-RED (#2114), which re-keyed the R9-F2.1 P3 storage check in tests/features.py off a race whose verdict is a property of the runner's BLAS kernel and onto the continuation's own gain, leaving that race measured and printed but not gating -- Price optimality is measured per kernel, and a shipped plan can be worse on the runner rather than in the solver. On this box the production multi-start ships a plan 0.307 SEK/day above the same solve warm-started from the half-price plan, where the Linux kernels of record measured the two within 0.0096 -- and the check's old bound, `j_plain <= j_seeded + 0.1`, was the measuring platform's own margin (+0.1000 on the container's Sandybridge, -0.2070 here) rather than a number below it, so that check was red on Accelerate only. For the D0 round: (1) record the kernel class beside every gap you report; CLAUDE.md rule 3 already states the premise -- solver floats do not reproduce across BLAS builds, so only a canonical environment can honestly record one -- and a gap from a start-placement race is a property of the runner as well as the solver. (2) A warm start from the best challenger is this dimension's own Method step 2, so this is the gap D0 is written to find and not a new technique; what is new is that its sign can depend on which basin the starts land in, which the fix's harness dev/audit/harnesses/r9_rc_blas_kernel_red_f21_p3.py measures and the archived finder instrument dev/audit/harnesses/k1725_blas_kernel_gap.py measured. (3) A single-kernel win is a candidate finding and not yet a sub-optimality: re-run it on a second kernel class, or state that the gap is kernel-relative. If it is real off this kernel the repair is in the multi-start's seeding, not in the check that printed it -- that check is green on the Linux kernels of record and on this box, and no bound on the race holds across both. Carry: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE (items 0112100b)",
+    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item 0d80b428: carry-201 entry 10 (file removed): the pull request fixing R9-ROW-STALE (#2111), which makes tools/audit/seat/record_row.py's `--plan` ask whether an existing row already tells the merge it records (`row_matches_merge`) rather than only whether a row exists (`has_row`), so the record-autofix beat now rewrites a stale row it previously left alone -- Constraint on record upkeep, and the owed action this lane leaves behind. The record-autofix beat (`tools/audit/seat/record_row.py`, driven by the `record-autofix` job) corrects ONLY the merges its tag window covers -- `--enumerate` reads `<last tag>..HEAD`, deliberately, since re-reading the whole history on every push is the cost it refuses -- so a row that went stale BEFORE the last tag is corrected by no automatic run, however correct the merge predicate now is. The next seat to do record upkeep owes one explicit run of the correction over a window that reaches the defect: `python3 -I tools/audit/seat/record_row.py --enumerate --since v6.7.13`, then `--plan` and `--apply` with `--root` at a worktree of origin/main. Measured at 7cd5a588c that plans the 103 stale rows this lane's fix exists to correct, plus the 2 merges that carry no row at all and are written rather than rewritten, one line per file, and re-plans to 0 -- the beat's own convergence, asked for over a window wide enough to include the rows. Nothing widens the tag window on its own, so this is a precondition on the record being correct rather than a recommendation: the fix converges the record inside the window it is run over, and the rows behind the tag are corrected only when a seat asks for them. Carry: R11-INTAKE-R10-RESIDUE (items 0d80b428)",
+    "From r10-closeout-prep-p2, body item c2bc6594: carry-201 entry 11 (file removed): the R10-RCA-NIGHTLY-PYSPY record (#2479), whose RCA is dev/audit/rca/nightly-ha-pyspy-timeout.md: the nightly-ha (stable) red is `hb:positive_control` failing when its single `py-spy dump --nonblocking` call times out at 30 s against CPython 3.14.6 / py-spy 0.4.2 -- Constraint, not an opportunity: `check_hb_positive_control` passes on ONE racy sample. +   "brief": "HOTFIX, Nightly is red and tests / nightly-status reddens the required gate on every code PR. Defect: Nightly run 43358851739 (workflow_dispatch at main e4ca2d940e), jobs nightly-ha (stable) 131667291235 and nightly-ha (2025.2.0) 131667291292, step 'Run ./.github/actions/nightly-ha' (python tests/nightly_ha.py), both print verbatim: 'FAIL a4:failed  [last_update_success=True]'; 'FAIL a4:unavailable  [50 still published: [entity ids, see the job log]]'; 'FAIL a4:buttons  [clickable after fail: [entity ids, see the job log]]'; 'FAIL a4:no_stale  [stale: [entity ids, see the job log]]'; 'FAILED: 5 of 65 checks: [a4:failed, a4:unavailable, a4:buttons, a4:no_stale, run:exit_status]'. Same five in run 43204237855 (head 4596f721f6, first red); last green nightly-ha: schedule run 43130126209 at 47788534da, 2026-11-12T00:49Z. Cause (established from the code path): PR #3454 (merge 2ced371c1a, R10-OUTSIDE-PRICE-OUTAGE-LAST-VALID, merged 2026-11-12T04:54Z, inside the window) changed coordinator._tibber_fetch_failed so a failed fetch raises UpdateFailed only when the last published prices no longer cover now (issues.note_price_outage, _known_prices_for); otherwise it bridges with a WARNING and the price_feed_stale repair. tests/nightly_ha.py _async_check_a4 breaks the fake Tibber with HTTP 500 right after a successful fetch of a 72-hour curve starting now-2h (_hourly_prices), so the cache covers now, the refresh succeeds, and every a4 check that presumes UpdateFailed fails. This is a harness defect against an intended, accepted product change -- not a product regression and not an HA/dependency change (both HA versions fail identically). Candidates ruled out: no other merge in 47788534da..4596f721f6 touches UpdateFailed/last_update_success (git diff of coordinator.py in the window), and the only entity.py/nightly_ha.py change (#3460) touches no availability path. Fix (tests/nightly_ha.py only; product code untouched; no revert of #3454, which would delete accepted working functionality): split A4 into two real-boundary phases. (1) New check a4:bridged: with the source broken while published prices cover now, last_update_success stays True, the price_feed_stale repair is in HA's issue registry, and entities stay available -- pins #3454's contract on real HA. (2) Exhaust the published prices at the counterparty, not by poking coordinator._prices: the fake server gains a mode that answers 200 with a curve that ended before now (e.g. _hourly_prices(now - 3 days)), one refresh takes it, then the 500 break runs the existing a4:failed/unavailable/buttons/no_stale unchanged, then restore and a4:recovered. Take 'before' records ahead of phase 2. Add a4:bridged to A4_INSIDE and to the tests/entities.py nightly roster/self-tests (the check_a4_* offline self-tests near the 'a4:failed fails when last_update_success stayed True' line) with a positive and a negative case. Update the nightly_ha.py module docstring (the Tibber bullet says the 500 always raises UpdateFailed; the A4 paragraph says it breaks Tibber with HTTP 500) and keep _a4_update_failed_traceback valid. If pull_prices refuses an expired curve as not-ok, state it and use the next real-boundary means; never set last_update_success or _prices from the harness. Verification and null control the body must show: a dispatched Nightly on the branch (gh workflow run nightly.yml --ref <branch>) where both nightly-ha jobs print 0 FAIL lines and a4:failed/unavailable/buttons/no_stale/recovered/bridged all ok; on main the same jobs print the five FAIL lines above (cite run 43358851739). Mutation control: with issues.note_price_outage forced to return False (bridging disabled) a4:bridged fails while a4:failed still passes; with the expired-curve step skipped a4:failed fails again with last_update_success=True. Must NOT change: anything under custom_components/, the other 60 checks' names and outcomes, log:no_integration_traceback ok, the a4 check functions' predicates. Root-cause trigger (defect-root-cause.md, red required gate): answer in the body why #3454's PR lane did not run nightly-ha (it runs only on schedule/dispatch), and whether a cheaper detector exists -- e.g. a pull-request rule that dispatches nightly-ha when a diff touches _tibber_fetch_failed/_fetch_tibber_prices or other names the A4 path reaches, or an offline entities.py self-test that drives _async_check_a4's premise against the coordinator -- with its standing cost, or record that none is worth building.",

+   "brief": "HOTFIX, blocks chain pull requests (triage seat triage-1113, proposal T4). Defect: tools/audit/seat/update_pr.sh never re-renders a body's generated figure blocks, so the body it hands on names a head the pull request no longer has and the pre-PR body check refuses with 'figure-block scope-line names another head'. Two paths carry it. (1) The moved-head path: the merge of the handoff code sha, the merge of origin/main and the own-row commit each move the head, and the body is pushed as written. (2) The body-only path (HPO_UPDATE_BODY_ONLY, run by the handoff arm of tools/audit/seat/cloud_relay.sh): the head is unmoved, but a handoff body's block names the authored code head while the pull request head is a merge of main, so body_patch.sh refuses on every relay pass. Measured instances on 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13: pull request 3510 (critical path) and pull request 3511 on the moved-head path, pull request 3511 again on the relay's body-only path on two passes, and pull request 3505's body patched by hand. The analogue is already in the tree: open_pr.sh's op_figures runs tools/pr/figure_block.py refresh on the body at each head it pushes, and a failed refresh refuses with nothing pushed. Fix: in update_pr.sh, when the body carries a figure block, run the same refresh at the head the run will push or patch, on both paths (before the push on the moved-head path, before body_patch.sh on the body-only path); a failed refresh refuses through pr_refuse and pushes or patches nothing; a body with no figure block is untouched. Reuse the refresh tool; do not add a second renderer. A local patch for path (1) only, with its macOS self-test logs, is at hpo-seats/r10s6/updpr-figfix (update_pr-figure-refresh.diff) under the orchestrator's home directory: read it, do not copy it blind, and extend it to path (2). Self-test, in pr_args.sh's self-test where update_pr.sh's arms live: a moved head with a figure block pushes a body whose block names the new head; a body-only run with a block naming another head patches a body naming the live head; a failing refresh pushes and patches nothing; a body without a block is byte-identical. Each new predicate line carries a mut tag its arm kills. Add one line to tools/audit/seat/instruments/update_pr.sh.md. The update-pr step of the instrument self-tests job will read the refresh tool, so record that step's entry in tools/ci/selftest_closures.json on Linux at current origin/main by union with the committed table (never replace), and show the scope check's summary line at zero refusals for that step. Null control: an update of a pull request whose body has no figure block runs exactly as today (same pushed body bytes). Before and after the body must show: the refusal reproduced at the merge base on a fixture body, and gone at the head.",

+   "brief": "HOTFIX, two opener refusals that were not the body's fault (root-cause seat R10-RCA-OPENER-REFUSALS, 2026-11-13). Part 1, open_pr.sh: two openers on one topic (a seat's hand run and open_driver.py's run) share the topic-keyed state dir, but the lock is keyed on the branch, and line 44 renames a second run to fix/<topic>-pr whenever a local fix/<topic> exists. The second run takes another lock, rewrites the shared body.md from handoff-body with no head note, then refuses in op_clear_leftover, and the first run's prepr step 7 refuses the overwritten body ('## Head does not name <merge>'). This happened three times tonight (03:20Z, 04:10Z, 06:10Z). Fix: line 44 does not rename when the topic's opener worktree is registered (git worktree list --porcelain lists it), so the second run takes the same lock and exits BUSY (75) before writing anything. Add a pr_args.sh self-test case run without BR_FIXED, with two openers on one topic. It must fail before the fix (the second run's rc is not 75 and body.md changes) and pass after (rc 75, body.md byte-identical). Null control: a lone run with a stale local fix/<topic> and no registered worktree still takes the -pr name. Tag the new clause with a mut: marker so deleting it fails the case. Also pin the head note: the main-moved fixture's stub pusher refuses a body whose ## Head does not name HEAD, and the note lines carry a mut: marker. Part 2, ci_predict.py: grader_preds runs carry_audit.py check in the ci_tree_run clone without the pr-contract step's round-roster fetch (pr-contract.yml, the step 'Every finding the body records names its destination'). The clone's origin/handoff/audit-r10-roster is therefore the shared repo's stale LOCAL branch, or absent, and a carry routed to a group appended to the round roster since then is predicted BAD-DEST where CI passes. In ci_tree_run, give the clone the round-roster refs the step's fetch yields. Self-test: a body routing to a group present on the root's origin/handoff/audit-r10-roster but absent from a stale local branch predicts BAD-DEST before the fix and nothing after. Null control: a group absent from both refs is still predicted BAD-DEST. Do not change carry_audit.py or the workflow. ## Before you return the verdict (P275)
Run `python3 -I tools/audit/carry_audit.py check --body <PR body file> --comments <file holding [{"id": 0, "body": <verdict.md text>}]>`. Give every reported item its own `Carry: <GROUP> | #<issue> | O<n> | dismissed: <reason>`;
an evidence line flagged by its wording takes `Carry: dismissed: evidence, not a finding`. Return the verdict only at rc 0.
